## Findings - Batch 1 (Branch A)

1. EXIT-CODE TRUTH vs CATALOG: with the canary `.AAM_TARGET_MOUNTED` present
   (production invariant), robocopy /MIR exits 3 on first sync (1 copied |
   2 extras - the /XF-excluded canary counts as a *named extra*) and 2 on
   steady-state reruns. classify_exit_code maps both to LAN_COMPLETE, so the
   PROGRAM contract holds; SCENARIO_CATALOG_V2 rows saying 'exit 1' are
   amended by this evidence.
2. LAN-05 is ENV-LIMITED: elevated admin + robocopy /ZB backup mode bypasses
   an icacls deny, so access-denied can only be reproduced under a real
   non-privileged service logon. Observed op recorded verbatim instead.
3. Zero program defects surfaced in this batch; one harness lesson recorded:
   walk_lan_destination has no /XF concept, so NAS-side comparisons must drop
   the canary row explicitly.

## Findings - Batch 2 (Branch A, LAN-06..10)

1. LAN-06 PROGRAM LIMIT (evidence-backed): under the production flag set
   (/V /TS /FP /NJH /NJS), a genuinely failed file (msvcrt byte-range lock,
   exit 11 = bit3 failures + extras) produces NO '** FAILED:' marker in the
   captured 100KB tail -> count_failed_lines()=0 and files_failed=0 in both
   result dict and run_history. Detection/classification/alerting still work
   (status=LAN_PARTIAL, error tail populated, healthy files landed, victim
   absent on NAS). Impact: dashboard/report 'Files Failed' counter reads 0 on
   exactly the runs where operators need it. Root cause: /NJS suppresses the
   job-summary section that carries per-file failure accounting; the /V body
   emits 'ERROR 33...' lines without the '** FAILED:' literal.
   Candidate remedies (NOT applied - decision pending): parse 'ERROR <code>'
   body lines, or drop /NJS.
2. LAN-07: >260 unicode path (332 chars incl devanagari component) is
   HANDLED natively by robocopy - LAN_COMPLETE exit 3, SHA256 exact. Catalog
   either/or branch resolved to HANDLED. Note: walk_lan_destination uses plain
   os.stat, so such entries on the NAS side would be skipped-with-warning by
   the H2 path - inventory blind spot for ultra-long paths, logged not fatal.
3. LAN-08: 50MB urandom blob mirrored SHA-exact across /Z /ZB (no truncation).
4. LAN-09/10: health gate refuses empty/missing source with exact reasons;
   pre-existing NAS sentinel survived -> proves SKIPPED-before-sync contract
   (no /MIR purge risk when source is bad).

## Findings - Batch 3 (Branch A, LAN-11..15)

1. LAN-11 (F8): timeout plumbing PROVEN live - caller-supplied 1s fired at
   wall 1.03s against a real C:\Windows /L listing (exit -1, 'Timeout after
   1s'). Hardcoded-300s era would not have fired; config bound ge=60 le=7200.
2. LAN-12 (G14): REAL post-sync walk failure (unreachable share, via the new
   H2 OSError path) -> snapshot returns None + CRITICAL log carrying the full
   H2 message. Pipeline keeps prior LAN status; DB row and metrics skipped
   this run only.
3. LAN-13: diff math exact (added1/modified1/removed0/unchanged9;
   files_copied=2, bytes_copied=930 matches expected sum) - dashboard
   extended_metrics inputs verified to the byte.
4. LAN-14 CATALOG CORRECTION #2: dest-only stray file under /MIR is PURGED
   and lands in extras accounting -> exit 3 -> LAN_COMPLETE with NO
   anomaly_details and NO error. Catalog's 'PARTIAL + anomaly tail' does NOT
   occur for plain orphans. anomaly_details (codes 4-7) requires genuine
   mismatches, not purged strays. Recorded as ANOMALY-RECORDED verdict.
5. LAN-15: two locked victims -> exit 11 PARTIAL, 2576-byte error tail,
   both victims absent on NAS, good file present. CONFIRMS Batch-2 limit:
   files_failed counter still 0, '** FAILED:' marker absent under /NJS -
   reproducible at n=2, so the counter gap is systemic, not a one-off.

## Cloud RT rerun with real GCS key (2026-08-23, post key+config+service restart)

- Key wired: deploy/keys/aam-demo-gcs-083e65c0184f.json (project aam-demo-gcs),
  config.yaml gcs_key_path updated; AamBackupAgent + AamWatchdog restarted
  (both ignore SCM STOP -> required taskkill /T /F recovery - new ops finding).
- test_rt_02_cloud_sync.py: 8 failed / 2 passed in 560s. ALL 8 failures share
  ONE root cause: googleapi 403 'storage.objects.list' denied on bucket
  aam-cloudbackup for SA rclone-backup-agent@aam-demo-gcs.iam.gserviceaccount.com.
- Interpretation: authentication SUCCEEDS (credential errors from the earlier
  runs are gone); AUTHORIZATION is missing at bucket IAM level. Affected:
  sync upload path, verify (rclone check listing), reporter lsjson,
  preflight dry-run list, purge/cleanup.
- Operator action required (bucket-level grant):
  roles/storage.objectAdmin to that SA on gs://aam-cloudbackup
  (gcloud storage buckets add-iam-policy-binding ...).
- pipe_01 + e2e test_4 NOT re-run yet: same bucket/prefix -> identical 403
  wall until IAM is fixed.

## Cloud RT FINAL (2026-08-23 16:16) - 12/12 PASS after config fix

Root cause chain (probe-evidenced, not guessed):
1. Missing key file -> fixed by operator-supplied aam-demo-gcs-083e65c0184f.json
   + config.yaml gcs_key_path update. Auth then succeeded.
2. Rerun still 10 failed / 2 passed with 229 identical 403
   storage.objects.list denials on gs://aam-cloudbackup.
3. Raw REST probe with the SA's own token proved:
   - SA CAN list its home project buckets (200).
   - Home project contains exactly ONE bucket: aam-backup-demo-innovizta
     (ASIA-SOUTH1, uniform bucket-level access ON).
   - gs://aam-cloudbackup -> 403 "(or it may not exist)" = bucket does not
     exist / invisible to SA. IAM grant could never fix a nonexistent name.
   - objects.list on aam-backup-demo-innovizta -> 200 (full access,
     FY26-27 objects visible).
4. Fix applied: config.yaml cloud.bucket = aam-backup-demo-innovizta,
   cloud.project_number = aam-demo-gcs (was placeholder aam-cloudbackup /
   aam-backup-2026). AamBackupAgent restarted (kill-tree recovery).
Result: test_rt_02_cloud_sync.py (10) + pipe_01_cloud_pipeline +
e2e test_4 = 12 passed, 0 failed, 69.29s, zero 403s.
Ops notes: both AAM services ignore SCM STOP -> every restart needs
taskkill /T /F on service PID; uv venv lacks pip (use uv pip install);
pyjwt+cryptography added to venv for probes.

## Findings - Batch 4 (Branch A, LAN-16..20) - BRANCH A CLOSED

1. LAN-16: missing source -> robocopy ERROR 2, exit 16 -> LAN_FAILED;
   classify_exit_code(16/-1) both LAN_FAILED. Contract solid.
2. LAN-17 (G8): >24h orphan robocopy log purged (removed=1), fresh kept.
   Age-gate exact.
3. LAN-18: shutdown contract proven live against TEST-NET dead IP -
   shutdown.exe genuinely invoked ('The entered computer name is not valid...'
   refusal recorded); disabled-config branch returns silently.
4. LAN-19 THREE honest FAILs before PASS, each a real discovery:
   a. wol.enabled=True with empty MAC -> immediate ValueError (F11 validation
      bites at pipeline entry - operator must provision MAC before enabling).
   b. Unreachable NAS IP -> pipeline waits FULL 300s for SMB then hard-aborts
      WolTimeout BEFORE sync (wake gate precedes everything; dead NAS = no
      backup attempt at all, by design).
   c. core/report.py:120 log message contains a NON-ASCII dash inside
      'send_on_failure <dash> skipping alert' - naive ASCII grep can never
      match it (bit the test probe; will bite any log-scraper too).
   Final PASS evidence: exit 11 PARTIAL; flow-level 'NAS shutdown SKIPPED'
   logged; zero shutdown attempts; alert gate reached (SMTP disabled on box);
   WoL wake OK vs live loopback NAS; run 43s end-to-end incl temp Prefect
   server.
5. LAN-20: /NC rejected with ValueError at build time; production command
   verified /MIR + R:3 W:10 + MT:4 present, /NC absent (22 flags).

semantics. All recorded verbatim above.

## Findings - Batch 5 (Branch B, CLOUD-01..05)

1. CLOUD-01/02: golden sync 3/3 objects (manifest-verified names) then rerun
   exit 9 -> CLOUD_NO_CHANGES_COMPLETE treated as SUCCESS. Contract exact.
2. CLOUD-03: two-probe preflight ok=True; wall recorded vs catalog <3s target.
3. CLOUD-04 (two harness corrections before PASS, both real discoveries):
   a. verify_cloud_integrity returns {verified, exit_code, error} ONLY - no
      per-file diff. Missing object -> verified=false, exit_code=1
      (_EXIT_MISMATCH), generic 'counts or sizes differ' message that does NOT
      name the lost file. Per-file naming is deferred to the flow manifest
      diff. Observability note: at this layer operators learn THAT loss
      happened, not WHAT.
   b. SECOND fancy-dash sighting in a user-facing error string ('Integrity
      mismatch <em-dash> source and GCS...') - reinforces the grep-hostile
      punctuation finding from Batch 4.
   F1 runtime-raise left wiring-evidenced (flow.py:474-496) - end-to-end
   trigger requires winning a post-sync race; refused to fake it.
4. Branch B isolation rule established: pipeline derives FY26-27 internally
   and rclone sync would purge live demo-bucket objects under it; all Branch B
   traffic pinned to E2E_TEST_FY prefix (purged at teardown). get_fy_prefix
   patch = project's own RT isolation pattern (pipe_01).

## Findings - Batch 6 (Branch B, CLOUD-06..10)

1. *** PROGRAM GAP - SILENT SUCCESS TRAP (CLOUD-06) ***: with a valid key but
   a NONEXISTENT bucket, rclone's initial listing fails fatally, transfers
   zero files, --error-on-no-transfer then exits 9 - and the classifier maps
   exit 9 unconditionally to CLOUD_NO_CHANGES_COMPLETE. A dead destination is
   reported as SUCCESS (ledger row: statuses=[FAILED, FAILED,
   NO_CHANGES_COMPLETE]). Missing/corrupt key files map correctly to
   CLOUD_FAILED exit 1 with reason-bearing tails. Candidate remedy (NOT
   applied): treat exit 9 as success ONLY when stderr carries no error-level
   entries; decision pending operator review.
2. CLOUD-07 retry machinery verified LIVE via retryable trigger (missing
   source root): 'Attempt 1/2' + 'Attempt 2/2' markers with ~30s
   (--retries-sleep) gaps between attempts; final exit 3 -> CLOUD_FAILED.
   KEY DISCOVERY PAIR with finding 1: fatal remote-listing errors BYPASS the
   attempt loop entirely (no markers, instant exit 9), while local-retryable
   errors engage it fully. Two failure classes, opposite retry behavior.
3. CLOUD-08 (G7): caller timeout=2 honored at wall 2.02s against a genuinely
   throttled transfer (512KB @ bwlimit=100B/s); exit -1 and the program's own
   message states resumability ('progress is preserved...next run continues').
4. CLOUD-09: 50MB upload under bwlimit=10M completed in 9.28s vs 5.0s
   theoretical floor - burst-then-cap behavior recorded verbatim.
5. CLOUD-10: storage_class=NEARLINE propagated all the way to OBJECT METADATA
   on Google's side (REST objects.get with SA token oracle):
   E2E_TEST_FY/class_probe.txt = NEARLINE.
6. Test-infra note: append scripts must build final content in memory before a
   single write - an earlier append+overwrite pair silently dropped batch-6
   classes (caught by pytest 'not found', fixed).

## Findings - Batch 7 (CLOUD-11..13 closes Branch B; HL-01..02 opens Branch C)

1. CLOUD-11 (two-phase redesign after one honest FAIL): first attempt seeded
   DB with DISK mtimes -> all 10 'unchanged' files tripped the >1.1s mtime
   guard because rclone/GCS object ModTime != disk mtime on fresh upload
   (files_copied=11, bytes=sum-of-all). Production-shaped truth: DB must hold
   CLOUD-SIDE state from a real prior upload. Phase-2 PASS: exactly 1 file /
   4096 bytes counted. Delta guards themselves verified exact.
   NOTE: pipeline never persists per-file entries after success - the
   production DB's file_entries only ever receive upserts from elsewhere;
   delta math depends entirely on that external writer keeping cloud-truth.
2. CLOUD-12: empty source -> HealthError 'appears empty' BEFORE sync; run row
   CLOUD_SKIPPED; zero rclone invocations; no VERIFY_FAILED confusion. Exact
   contract per catalog.
3. CLOUD-13: with deploy/bin/rclone.exe renamed + PATH scrubbed:
   resolve_binary->None; pre_backup_health raises 'rclone not found in PATH';
   direct run_cloud_sync -> CLOUD_FAILED exit -1 error 'rclone not found'
   (FileNotFoundError caught and mapped to the -1 resumable contract).
   Binary restored byte-for-byte (78717440) after probe - verified.
4. HL-01/HL-02: exact catalog reasons ('not accessible' / 'appears empty')
   via pre_backup_health direct calls.

*** CRITICAL TEST-SUITE FINDING (disclosed to operator, NOT self-fixed) ***:
rt_06 pipe_01 / e2e test_4 patch core.fy_router.get_fy_prefix, but flow.py:29
binds get_fy_prefix DIRECTLY (from-import), so their patch is a NO-OP. Their
pipeline runs therefore targeted the REAL FY26-27 prefix and rclone sync
PURGED pre-existing objects under it during today's successful cloud rerun
(bucket now holds only their five 1024B test files). Correct isolation is to
patch flow.get_fy_prefix (the consumer binding) as Batch-7 scenarios do.
Operator decision needed: fix rt_06/e2e patch target + whether FY26-27 demo
objects must be re-created.

## Findings - Batch 8 (HL-03..07)

1. HL-03: real icacls DENY ACE (current user, OI+CI+F) WAS honored by plain
   Python iterdir even under elevated admin - opposite of the LAN-05 finding
   where robocopy /ZB bypassed ACLs. Gate refused with exact 'permission
   denied' reason. ACL reset + dir removed in finally.
2. HL-04: threshold driven through the REAL min_free_source_gb parameter
   (10 TiB minimum vs actual 140.9 GB free): reason string carries BOTH
   numbers verbatim -> config-to-message fidelity proven.
3. HL-05: deploy/bin/rclone.exe rename + PATH scrub -> resolve_binary None,
   gate 'rclone not found in PATH'; binary restored byte-for-byte.
4. HL-06: zero filesystem changes needed - robocopy resolves only via System32
   PATH here, so in-process PATH scrub alone produced 'robocopy not found in
   PATH'. Cleanest possible reversal.
5. HL-07: missing key -> 'GCS key file not found: <path>'; 0-byte key ->
   'GCS key file is empty: <path>'. Check-order note: clock-skew network probe
   sits AFTER key check - a bad key fails fast with no network dependency.
6. Health branch observation: every refusal reason embeds the offending path
   and/or numbers - operator-actionable messages throughout this module.

## Findings - Batch 11 (WOL-07..09 closes Branch D; FY-01..02 opens Branch E)

1. WOL-07: broadcast override validated at construction - "Invalid
   broadcast_address IPv4 '999.999.0.1'. Set to empty string to auto-derive"
   + empty-string control accepted.
2. WOL-08 ANOMALY-RECORDED: DNS-failure path safe (gaierror -> False) BUT the
   except clause only catches OSError - port=99999 raises OverflowError that
   ESCAPES _smb_port_open. Config validators prevent bad ports upstream, yet
   the helper's own contract ("more reliable than ping") leaks an exception
   type it claims to own. Candidate remedy: widen to (OSError, OverflowError,
   ValueError) or clamp port range.
3. WOL-09 ANOMALY-RECORDED: round loop + dual per-target OSError guards
   verified; unparseable MAC probe -> ValueError ('non-hexadecimal...fromhex')
   escapes the warn-and-continue net. Same shape as WOL-08 finding: narrow
   excepts in wake layer vs richer failure surface of dependencies.
4. FY-01: production paths carry current FY26-27 -> detect_rollover False;
   daily/bot rollover check is a verified no-op today.
5. FY-02 detection half: previous-FY paths (FY25-26) vs computed FY26-27 ->
   True. rollover() EXECUTION deliberately deferred to a sandboxed batch
   (rewrites config.yaml, touches both destinations, runs final backups).

## Findings - Batch 12 (FY-03..07)

1. FY-03: no-FY paths -> False + exact warning "rollover is disabled by
   configuration..." (G2 opt-out contract).
2. FY-04: BOTH sides refused at real from_yaml load - "uses a 4-digit FY name
   ('FY2026-27')... silently disables rollover forever" with the offending
   path key named (source_drive / lan_destination).
3. FY-05: mismatch guard fires with full "CRITICAL DATA LOSS PREVENTION"
   text naming both FY labels and refusing startup.
   Harness note (3 iterations): sandbox YAML is now a CLONE of the real
   config.yaml with only the two path lines rewritten (lambda-based re.sub
   to survive backslash templates). Side discovery: validator pecking order
   at load = WolConfig MAC -> CloudConfig bucket -> DashboardConfig api_key
   -> FY guards; each failure masks deeper ones until fixed.
4. FY-06: run_final_backup happy path vs LIVE destinations -> (True, True);
   NAS mirror contained exactly closing_fy.txt - production /XF set correctly
   EXCLUDES the .AAM_TARGET_MOUNTED sentinel from mirrors (bonus verification).
5. FY-07: broken cloud creds -> (False, True) = BLOCKED. Oracle probe pinned
   the failure class: typo-bucket WITH valid source exits 1 CLOUD_FAILED -
   NOT the Batch-6 exit-9 trap (that needs source-side listing to also fail).
   Rollover's BLOCKED gate therefore holds for this class; raise-site
   fy_rollover.py:442-444 read-verified.
6. PRODUCTION-FIDELITY NOTE: run_final_backup honors lan.enabled / cloud.enabled
   flags - with prod config LAN-disabled it skips the leg and returns lan_ok
   False. Correct behavior; scenarios now enable legs explicitly in sandbox.

## Findings - Batch 13 (FY-08..12)

1. FY-08: FULL rollover() blocked contract proven live - unreachable NAS ->
   RolloverError "FY rollover blocked: final backup failed for LAN";
   sandbox yaml byte-identical after (retry-next-day); zero new-FY folders.
   CRITICAL discovery on the way: with prod config's lan.enabled=false the
   failing NAS is NOT required - rollover proceeds and rewrites paths. The
   enabled flags are load-bearing safety, not decoration.
2. FY-09: cloud disabled + LAN failing -> error names LAN ONLY; disabled
   cloud never enters the required list ("respects enabled list" contract).
   Side-discovery of a real validator: "At least one destination (lan or
   cloud) must be enabled" refuses configs that disable both.
3. FY-10: server-side ARCHIVE transition verified to OBJECT METADATA via
   REST oracle: E2E_ARCHIVE_FY/to_archive.txt = ARCHIVE, archive_ok True,
   zero warnings. DEPLOYMENT PREREQUISITE found live: bundled
   deploy/bin/google-cloud-sdk gcloud.cmd requires CLOUDSDK_PYTHON when no
   system Python exists - without it every ARCHIVE transition silently no-ops
   (non-blocking by design) leaving objects STANDARD. Recommend install
   script set CLOUDSDK_PYTHON machine-wide.
4. FY-11: absent key -> archive_ok False + WARNING only; rollover unaffected.
5. FY-12: source folder created; LAN mkdir against dead share -> ERROR +
   exact "ACTION REQUIRED: Manually create" guidance; created dict has
   source only; non-blocking confirmed.
6. Test-suite hygiene: accidental double-append of Batch-12 classes deduped;
   sandbox-config helper now supports section-scoped, callable-safe YAML edits.

1. SCH-03 recorded WIRING-EVIDENCED alongside: this Prefect version's Cron
   object carries no catchup attribute; missed-slot semantics are governed by
   the server schema (CronSchedule default) - no catch-up replay observed on
   this deployment; DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE documents gap-as-expected.
2. SCH-04: real PENDING run created on the LIVE Prefect server (clean-env
   subprocess, conftest forces ephemeral in-process) -> boot janitor
   cancelled it: final state CANCELLED + "Cancelled orphaned" printed.
3. SCH-05: backup.lock written with LIVE pid (self) -> sweep printed
   "Skipping RUNNING flows"; RUNNING stayed RUNNING; PENDING -> CANCELLED.
   F13 two-tier semantics proven against the live server.
4. SCH-06: lock with killed PID -> "Stale backup lock ... cleaning up",
   lock file deleted, sweep proceeds unshielded.

   live server via client read (created at launch boot); tag limit creation
   path read-verified in _ensure_concurrency_limit.
6. INFRA NOTE: tests/conftest.py forces PREFECT_TEST_MODE=1 process-wide ->
   any in-process Prefect client goes ephemeral. Server-touching scenarios
   therefore execute inside a clean-env subprocess pinned to the production
   API URL; orphan runs use a local dummy flow so no backup pipeline can be
   triggered accidentally.
7. API note: this prefect version's create_flow_run takes a Flow OBJECT and
   internally reads flow.version - server-returned Flow models lack that
   attribute, so ad-hoc runs must come from a client-side @flow definition.

## Findings - Batch 17 (SCH-08..12)

1. SCH-08: write_lock sits INSIDE the concurrency slot (source order
   verified); two-process hammering (40 writes each) -> atomic mkstemp+
   os.replace held up: final lock always parseable 'PID:create_epoch_float'.
   Two honest FAILs before PASS taught: (a) write_lock requires a Path (str
   crashes - caller contract); (b) losing writer under contention can hit
   transient OSError which the CALLER logs-and-continues without lock - by
   design, and holder_alive=False post-exit is CORRECT semantics.
2. SCH-09: _stable_run_id stable within a real flow context (proven inside a
   live @flow probe; outside context it intentionally falls back to fresh
   UUIDs). DB layer: record_run_history ON CONFLICT(run_id) DO UPDATE ->
   exactly ONE row with FINAL status/error after duplicate insert.
3. SCH-10: recorded duration_seconds=5.0 from monotonic pair while wall-clock
   started_at claimed year-2000 (old formula would yield ~8e8s). F16 immune
   to NTP jumps - proven to the row.
4. SCH-11: backup.fn(mode='banana') -> "Invalid mode 'banana'. Must be one
   of: ['all', 'cloud', 'lan']" before any config work.
5. SCH-12: both destinations disabled -> AppConfig refuses at load with
   "At least one destination (lan or cloud) must be enabled".

## Findings - Batch 19 (DB-01..05, opens Branch G)

   db_meta (+sqlite_sequence); schema_version=1. DDL contract exact.

   the active set is untouched (correct); the cross-mode survivor case needs
   it ABSENT from the live cloud manifest. Final proof: pruned=3; p4/p5

## Findings - Batch 20 (DB-06..10)

1. DB-06: 1200-path delete across three 500-chunks -> doomed gone, keepers
   intact; variable-limit safety verified at scale.
2. DB-07: duplicate insert_run same run_id -> ONE row, final status/exit
   visible (LAN_FAILED/11 -> LAN_COMPLETE/3). Retry-proof history.
3. DB-08: last_successful_run ignores newer FAILED/PARTIAL - returns the
   older COMPLETE (LIKE %_COMPLETE filter verified against a 3-run ladder).
4. DB-09: purge_old_runs(90d) removed all pre-cutoff history, kept current;
   VACUUM branch fired (threshold forced to 1) and collapsed freelist to 0.
   file_entries untouched by run-log purge (G14 split responsibilities).
5. DB-10: 4 in-process threads + a second ManifestDB client on the same file
   wrote concurrently under WAL + busy_timeout=30000ms: zero errors, exactly
   241 rows. First probe attempt used raw SQL INSERT missing NOT NULL
   created_at -> IntegrityError; switched to the program API which is the
   realistic dashboard path anyway.
6. Suite hygiene: append-script overwrite bug resurfaced and was fixed by
   rebuilding branch_g from both source blocks; DB-05 correction re-applied.

## Findings - Batch 21 (DB-11..15)

1. DB-11: record_sync_results handles BOTH key dialects (rclone Path/Size/
   ModTime and walk path/size/mtime) in one call; stale cloud-only entry is
   fully DELETED (both-null GC) while removed-list entry is deleted by
   explicit request.vanished_cloud_nulled=False + vanished_fully_deleted=True
   IS the correct terminal state for a single-leg orphan.
2. DB-12: record_run_history persists CLOUD_FAILED and CLOUD_SKIPPED
   verbatim (returns True); F2 truthfulness relies on pipeline finally-block
   choosing the right status - persistence layer records whatever it gets.
3. DB-13: md5_checksum survives an md5-less re-upsert (COALESCE semantics).
4. DB-14: file_count(status_field='bad') -> ValueError "must be one of"
   naming the offending field; dynamic-SQL whitelist holds.
5. DB-15: after 500-row bulk write, wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) drops -wal to
   0 bytes -> no unbounded WAL growth across daily runs.
6. BRANCH G: 15/17 green. Remaining: DB-16 get_synced_paths mode guard,
   DB-17 update_checksums bulk.

## Findings - Batch 23 (UI-01..05, opens Branch H)

1. UI-01: /health unauthenticated -> 200 healthy + source_accessible=true
   (real Path.exists against production source drive).
2. UI-02: dual-mode auth block verified - browser Accept:text/html -> 303
   Location /login; JSON client -> 401 Unauthorized.
3. UI-03: login full contract - wrong key bounces to /login?error; correct
   key -> 303 to / with session cookie: token_hex(32)=64 chars, HttpOnly,
   SameSite=lax, Max-Age=86400. Cookie grants subsequent access.
4. UI-04: trigger bucket exhausted exactly at _RATE_MAX_TRIGGER=5 -> 6th
   POST returns 429 "Rate limit exceeded". Attempts 1-5 surfaced as 500
   trigger_failed under ephemeral Prefect (no deployments) and STILL consumed
   the window - rate limiter counts attempts, not successes (correct for
   brute-force protection).
5. UI-05 WIRING-EVIDENCED + partial live: _is_running("cloud") executes a
   real Prefect query (False on idle); source order verified that the gate
   precedes arun_deployment. Producing the True state live requires an
   in-flight backup on production paths - deferred to operator-supervised run.
6. BRANCH H: 5/15.

## Findings - Batch 24 (UI-06..10)

1. UI-06: fail path LIVE - missing deployment under ephemeral Prefect ->
   500 {"status":"trigger_failed"} with detail; G15 await wiring verified in
   source (success returns 200 triggered + flow_run.id). Executing the
   success branch here would launch a real production backup - left for an
   operator-supervised window.
2. UI-07: TTL reload + F13 eviction verified by pointing the REAL loader
   (models.config.CONFIG_PATH seam) at a sandbox yaml, then swapping content
   to config-B and expiring _config_loaded_at: _cfg() picked up new yaml and
   get_db() rebound to the NEW database path under the same RLock.
3. UI-08: /report/weekly with absent DB -> exact 503 body "No database found.
   Run a backup first." (catalog contract verbatim).
4. UI-09: manual weekly email landed on 500 SMTP-error branch (placeholders on
   this box) - one of the three documented outcomes; delivery itself out of
   scope until SMTP creds arrive.
5. UI-10: 20 concurrent /status requests while a refresher continuously
   expired the config TTL -> zero 5xx, zero transport errors; RLock
   serialized lifecycle exactly as F13 describes. Harness note: httpx
   ASGITransport is async-only; per-thread TestClient used instead.
6. BRANCH H: 10/15. Remaining: UI-11..15 (/status contract, login/report
   rate limits, session TTL expiry, cron_to_human display).

1. UI-11: /status full contract against PRODUCTION runtime DB - firm,
   fy_prefix=FY26-27, human schedules ("Daily at 22:00 Kolkata"),
   cloud/lan {running,last_run,last_success}, health.source_free_gb,
   recent_runs list. Dashboard reads live data end-to-end.
2. UI-12: login bucket caps at 10; 11th attempt -> 429 (brute-force guard
   runs before key validation). First pass failed due to cross-test bucket
   pollution from UI-03 logins in the same process - fixed with an autouse
   fixture that clears _RATE_LIMITS/_sessions per scenario (fresh-window
   simulation, mirroring a restarted dashboard).
3. UI-13: report download bucket independent of trigger/login; 11th GET ->
   429.
4. UI-14: session aged 25h -> _validate_session returns False AND deletes
   the token (stolen-cookie window closed, not just rejected).
5. UI-15: cron_to_human renders Daily/Every Monday/1st-of-month exactly;
   F14 ordinal fix confirmed (11th/13th, not 11st/13rd).
6. *** BRANCH H CLOSED: 15/15. ***

## Findings - Batch 26 (REP-01..05, opens Branch I)

1. REP-01: full MIME contract verified by DECODING the captured message -
   subject decodes to "Backup Failure Alert - AAM Associates (CLOUD)" (the
   raw header is RFC2047 =?utf-8?q?...?= with em-dash =E2=80=94 - FOURTH
   fancy-punctuation sighting, this time in an email subject); html body has
   <table> + "[TRUNCATED - SEE ATTACHMENT"; attachment failure_details.txt
   carries exactly 1500 bytes.
2. REP-02: send_on_failure=false -> False + skip log + ZERO SMTP traffic.
3. REP-03: scripted 535 on AUTH -> permanent error logged after exactly ONE
   session; no retry storm against hopeless credentials.
4. REP-04: connection dropped mid-handshake -> retried once after ~10s
   backoff window and SUCCEEDED (True) - transient self-healing live.
5. REP-05: all three attempts dropped -> False after exponential gaps
   (27.5s >= (10+20)*0.8 jitter floor). Alert loss is loud, not silent.
6. Harness: local SMTP server needed THREE protocol fixes before the program
   would talk to it - _reply() method missing entirely, EHLO must advertise
   AUTH mechanisms for smtplib.login(), STARTTLS advertisement + runtime
   self-signed TLS cert because report.py calls starttls() on every non-465
   port. The program's SMTP behavior was correct throughout; every failure
   was my fake server's fault - which is itself evidence of robustness.

## Findings - Batch 28 (WD-01..05, opens Branch J)

1. WD-01 live: API health OK, AamBackupAgent RUNNING, no transfer -> green
   path confirmed with real signals.
2. WD-02 wiring: FAILURE_THRESHOLD=5 @60s interval + below-threshold sleep
   branch verified verbatim.
3. WD-03: transfer detection works on process NAME (spawned a renamed
   interpreter as rclone.exe -> detected; killed -> undetected). 240-deferral
   zombie cap + forced-restart branch wired verbatim.
   NOTE: none_running_initially=False at scenario start revealed an ORPHANED
   rclone/robocopy process on this box (leftover from interrupted test runs)
   - flagged for cleanup.
4. WD-04: lock triage exact - live PID True; dead-PID False + Stale warning;
   corrupt payloads ('not_a_pid', '-999') degrade to False WITHOUT crashing
   (G6). Grace comes from read_lock_alive's internal handling rather than a
   watchdog-level warning - behavior correct either way.
5. WD-05 wiring: sc stop command construction, WATCHED_SERVICE constant,
   NSSM ~30s restart note, failureflag awareness all verbatim; behavioral
   cross-ref = today's manual restart incidents recovered cleanly.
6. Branch J loop-level timing (5-min outage drill, 30-min stale cap, 8h
   zombie cap) remains wiring-evidenced - full-duration drills would take
   hours of deliberate downtime.

Correction (WD-03): key naming confusion - none_running_initially stores
_transfer_process_running() OUTPUT; False = nothing detected at start (GOOD).
Sequence was clean: none -> renamed rclone.exe detected -> none after kill.
No orphaned processes exist on this box (tasklist verified). No cleanup needed.

## Findings - Batch 29 (WD-06..10)

1. WD-06: transitioning states (START/STOP_PENDING, PAUSED*) reset the
   failure counter and sleep 60s - never kicked mid-restart (F6 rationale).
   Live state read: AamPrefectServer=RUNNING.
2. WD-07: circuit breaker driven live through _start_allowed - first 3
   allowed, 4th blocked; MAX_STARTS_PER_WINDOW=3 + CRITICAL manual-intervention
   message wired before every sc start.
3. WD-08: recovery pop reopens the breaker - blocked_at_cap=True then
   allowed_after_recovery_pop=True using the exact main() pop line.

## FINAL FULL-CATALOG VERIFICATION RUN

Command: pytest over all ten scenario files (branches A..J), real-hardware gate on.
RESULT: 146 passed, 1 skipped (LAN-05 ENV-LIMITED by design - admin+ZB
bypasses ACLs; needs real non-privileged service logon to reproduce), 0 failed.

Fixes applied during verification (all test-harness, zero program changes):
1. branch_e _write_yaml restored to config.yaml CLONE approach (a stale
   hand-built YAML copy had resurfaced via earlier append/dedup churn).
2. FY-06/07: lan.enabled=True sandbox lines + canary-exclusion assert
   re-applied (lost in same churn).
3. branch_a STRAY IMPORT BLOCK removed: a foreign header had appended
   'from core.health import HealthError' at module level mid-file,
   REBINDING the name and breaking LAN-03's except matching. Root cause of
   the phantom LAN-03 failures across full runs. LAN-03 now green again.
4. SCH-05 make_run hardened against live-server 409 races (retry with fresh
   dummy run until state sticks).

CATALOG STATUS: every scenario in SCENARIO_CATALOG_V2.md has been executed at
